# NOTES (coa3): the Companies Act 2006 (United Kingdom), Parts 8 and 9, ss 112 to 153 (lines 1414-2021)

Part agent `coa3`, run `dst-coa3`, written on 2026-11-13 by Claude Sonnet 5.5 on the stable binary 1.6.110 (main, e51181d, 2026-11-11), compared with 1.6.109 (main, 4c887e0, 2026-11-04).
This file carries the sections the brief asks for, for my ranges only; the lead writes `README.md`, `SOURCE-LICENSE.md`, `encoding.json` and `subject.json`.

## What this is and what it rests on

- **The text.** `ukpga-2006-46.txt` is a plain-text rendering of the Act made by a converter script from legislation.gov.uk (Open Government Licence v3.0). It is not the official text, and nothing here is "verified against the official text". Every quotation is copied by `srcrefs.py quote` from that file, and `python3 -I srcrefs.py check --strict` on my files reports 0 problems (566 quote lines, 228 runs, 297 locators).
- **The two layers.** Layer 1 is the law, objectively: five modules `coa3-part8-members.l4` (ss 112 to 113J), `coa3-part8-inspection.l4` (ss 114 to 128ZA, Chapter 2A), `coa3-part8-branch.l4` (Chapter 3), `coa3-part8-subsidiary.l4` (Chapter 4) and `coa3-part9-rights.l4` (Part 9), each with a tests file. Layer 2 is one thin seat layer, `coa3-seat.l4`, with its cases `coa3-cases-seat.l4`.
- **Target.** The stable `l4` (main). No section `GIVEN`, no `REFUSE`, no sets, no `YMD`, no `TYPICALLY`, no opaque `DECLARE`; dates are `Date day month year`; every `NOT` is bracketed; every `#ASSERT` is on one line. Both builds read every file with the same counts, and the regulative traces print the same outcomes on both.
- **Method.** Quotes are expanded from the raw text by a script (`work/build.py`, not shipped); the coverage table is built from the text by `work/coverage.py` over a hand-written map; the seat file is generated from a spec (`work/gen_seat.py`). Expected values in every tests file and every case are written by hand from the text, before the run, and sit beside the assertion.

## Seat

An individual who is, or is about to become, a director, a company secretary or a member (shareholder) of a company registered under the Act, or who deals with one, asking on facts they can state what the Act requires or lets them do, what rights they have against the company or its directors, what they must disclose, file, approve or vote on and by when, and what follows (liability, offences, penalties) if it is not done.
(Assumed, not ruled: the Act is addressed to companies, so the individual is whoever stands in a company's management or ownership.)
There is no seat for the registrar, the court or the Secretary of State: what a section says they do appears only as what it means for the person (for example `Under section 117(3), can the court order me to pay the company's costs`).
`coa3-seat.l4` has 106 questions, each named "Under section N," with the Act's own numbering, each with `@export` and an `@desc` on every input worded as the person would be asked, grouped under `§` headings in the text's order; a heading's comment says where a group arises only in a situation the person may not be in (for example the overseas branch register questions).
Where an answer is another part's (s 1121 "in default", s 853E(6) "relevant market", s 1159 "subsidiary", s 1173(1) "UK regulated market", Schedule 5), the question asks the person the fact that answers it: a yes or no they state, or "yes", "no" or "not known" where they may not have it.
Penalty questions answer with the typed `Penalty`; the pounds that a level on the standard scale or the statutory maximum stands for are not in the Act and are not asked.
Where the text shows two versions of a provision (s 113(2); ss 113J and 115) there is no seat question on s 113(2) itself (the one that touches the register's contents, s 113(2A), belongs with the second version), and both index sections are exposed, `Under section 113J(1)` and `Under section 115(1)`; a caller who must choose should prefer s 113J (forks F1, F2).

## Where this file and the brief differ from PLAN.md

- **`coa-definitions` is not imported.** The brief (line 17) says layer 1 IMPORTs both `coa-core.l4` and `coa-definitions.l4`; PLAN.md section 4.1 says a part cannot import `coa-definitions` and takes the keel's answers as seam inputs. I followed PLAN: layer 1 imports `prelude`, `daydate` and `coa-core` only (and my own modules), because taking the keel's answers as inputs is the merge contract every other part follows and nothing in my ranges needs a type that only `coa-definitions` declares. The lead may overrule this.
- **The text prints some provisions twice** (s 113(2); the two "Index of members" sections). PLAN.md does not mention it; see F1, F2 and finding 1.
- Everything else follows the brief. Where PLAN names an input (`s 1(1) the body is a company`, `s 1121 the officer is in default`, `s 1159(1) ...`, `s 3(2) ...`) I used its name exactly.

## Rulings applied (copied word for word from the brief)


These rulings set this corpus's drafting conventions, which each row follows and flags; L4 itself takes no position on them.
The rule of 2026-10-29 for what `JUST FALSE` and `NOTHING` mean is the convention the rows written since then follow (applied in legalese/commons#30 and #37); it was assumed, not ruled as a whole, and the rulings below settle parts of it.
In short, a function answers `JUST FALSE` only where the text itself says no to the case in its own words: a "means" definition, a condition the text introduces with "if", "only if", "as long as" or "unless" (and plain variants such as "while", "until" and "except"), a list the text closes with "only", a flat negative ("does not", "shall not", "may not").
Everywhere else it answers `NOTHING`: where the text lists without closing the list, says "includes", leaves a term undefined, is silent, points outside the sources, or limits a grant or prohibition in its own wording and the case falls outside the limit.
The wording is copied from section 2 of `docs/design-question-false-or-nothing.md`, which also states the two readings left open for a lawyer.
Each ruling is recorded again, word for word, in the `NOTES.md` of each row that carries that rule's `NOTHING-kind` tags: in its fork register where it has one, and in a section of its own where it has none.

- **E1** (bench `commons-negation-1030`; should a legal effect be one definition gathered from every section that gives it?): option B, as guidance for gathering a legal effect's routes by hand only, with no pilot run; option A (gathering by bare name) is withdrawn as a direction for the language, not deferred; option C (per-section answers) applies everywhere else; the Magna Carta pilot in the printed recommendation (chapters 2, 3, 4, 5, 37, 43, 46 and 53, with a blind second reader) is not to be run. The maintainer's lean, verbatim: "B but generally uncomfortable with this much magic because it takes the language from unopinionated to opinionated in ways that are hard to teach an l4 novice." the maintainer's note, verbatim: "Fundamentally I feel like we are trying to make the language do work that the human l4 drafter should do. Too much magic. Can’t we just decline to be this opinionated and say the programmer needs to do this thinking?" Marked 2026-11-03T23:19:02.812Z, ruled 2026-11-04. Bench: https://claude.ai/artifact/49q6k2H5xtuJqy5ttkwZ23
- **N2** (bench `commons-negation-1030`; does a flat negative count as the text's no?): option A as printed: flat negatives count as a no, and where the text names the bearer the prohibition (`SHANT`) is derived from that answer; the Hohfeld kinds stay in each row's "Jural relations" table; option C's wording (the kind typed in the person-facing answer) comes only after a lawyer confirms the typing, decided together with the S/E choice for how "not covered" is said. The maintainer's lean, verbatim: "Why are we even trying to do this work? Some of these rules are metatextual and require more homoiconicity than we currently have in L4." the maintainer's note: none written. Marked 2026-11-03T23:22:49.971Z, ruled 2026-11-04. Bench: https://claude.ai/artifact/49q6k2H5xtuJqy5ttkwZ23
- **Periods, "where" and scope limits** (a question raised on 2026-11-05 while checking an encoding of the Retirement and Re-employment Act 1993, not a bench): (a) a period the text fixes in its own words ("within one month", "begins from ... until the employee attains the age", "on the last day of employment"): outside the period the answer is `JUST FALSE`; (b) a "where ..." condition: when it fails the answer is `JUST FALSE`; (c) a scope limit with no "only" (for example "below" the minimum age): outside the scope the answer is `NOTHING`. The maintainer's words, verbatim apart from the trigger word that ended the message: "i agree with your lean: no for a and b; nothing for c." Typed 2026-11-05T04:30:00.322Z, ruled 2026-11-05.
- **Delegated definitions, defences and "until" bars** (questions raised on 2026-11-05 while checking encodings of the Proceeds of Crime Act 2002, the Terrorism Act 2000 and the Singapore Sale of Goods Act 1979, not a bench): (a) a definition the text itself hands to a list ("Schedule 9 has effect for determining what is a business in the regulated sector"): when no head in the list holds, the answer is `JUST FALSE`; a bare list with no such hand-off still answers `NOTHING`; (b) "it is a defence ... to prove that X": when X is not proved, the answer is `JUST FALSE` for that provision's defence, worded "this section gives no defence on these facts" and never "no defence at all"; (c) "when", "upon" and "whenever" count as "where" under rule (b) of the periods ruling above; (d) "the property does not pass until X": encode the bar ("the property cannot yet pass": `JUST TRUE` before X, `JUST FALSE` after X) and never claim that the property has passed. The maintainer approved these four readings as put to him by the general-manager session, in the word NOTHINGBURGER; his note on (d), verbatim apart from the bracket and the trigger word that ended the message: "in the case of Q5 [the "until" bar] i hope we are using the deontic mechanisms to examine a trace." The note is a request about how (d) is modelled, not part of the ruling. Typed 2026-11-05T06:04:54.553Z, ruled 2026-11-05.
- **Questions with no convention: decide case by case (the maintainer, typed 2026-11-05T10:25:24Z).** Six questions the rulings above leave open are decided site by site, at encoding time, on the text's own words and the encoder's best judgment: an "unless" or "except" once it is met; a bare "must" not done; "listed in Schedule N"; a duty whose "if" or "where" is unmet; "(other than X)"; a missing event in the state ledger. Each such site gets one fork line (the provision, the function, the answer taken, the other reading) for the human reviewer to overrule, and the rulings above still govern whatever they cover. The maintainer's words, asked of the session "Encodings": "re Encoding Conventions I believe I had a short discussion with the coordinator where I basically said I agreed with its \"lean\"s -- does that mean we can retire the bench card or should I go over it still? In short I feel like these decisions need to be made case-by-case at encoding time, and you should go with your gut most of the time; and if the encoding turns out to be wrong, well, that's where the human review comes in." The bench that carried the six questions ("Encoding Conventions", artifact Hpg2k9HqExJN5zdtBYiJ72, collection `encodings-conventions-1105`) was retired unmarked, so it holds no marks.
- **Lists:** write a list of strings as a vertical `LIST` block, one string per line, not with `•` bullets (those are a parse error on the stable binary).

### Seat audit of 2026-11-12

The lead asked every part to read each seat question's name and `@export` text against the layer-1 function its body calls (a name that asks one thing while the function answers another: a permission against a bar, "need" against "need not", "where" against "whether").
I read all 106 questions of `coa3-seat.l4`. **6 mismatched: 4 by direction and 2 by subject.** The other 100 ask what their function answers. Negating the answer was not used as a fix (a NOTHING or a JUST FALSE is not a grant); each question was retitled, with its `@export` text, to ask what the function answers, and the facts and expected values of its cases did not change.

| kind | old name | new name (also the `@export` text, with a closing "?") | what the function answers |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| direction | Under section 112(4), does the wrong form of my name in the register stop me being a member | Under section 112(4), do I still become a member although my name is entered in the wrong form | `s 112(4) the person becomes a member under s 112(2) although the entered name is not in the form required by s 113A`: JUST TRUE means the membership is kept, the opposite of "stop me" |
| direction | Under section 124(1), need the company enter the shares it bought back in the register | Under section 124(1), is the company excused from complying with section 113 for the shares it bought back | `s 124(1)(a) the requirements of section 113 need not be complied with`: JUST TRUE means excused, the opposite of "need ... enter" |
| direction | Under section 146(5), need the company act on my nomination | Under section 146(5), is the company excused from acting on my nomination | `s 146(5) the company need not act on the nomination`: JUST TRUE means excused |
| direction | Under section 150(4), need the company send the document to the person I nominated | Under section 150(4), is the company excused from sending the document to the person I nominated | `s 150(4)(a) the company need not send or supply the document or information to the nominated person`: JUST TRUE means excused |
| subject | Under section 117(1), has the company answered my request in time | Under section 117(1), has the company complied with my request or applied to the court within five working days | `s 117(1) the company acted within five working days`: the duty is to comply or to apply to the court, and applying is not answering; the plain input was renamed to match |
| subject | Under section 135(2), where must the entries go when the company discontinues an overseas branch register | Under section 135(2), does transferring the entries to this place meet the duty when the company discontinues an overseas branch register | `s 135(2) all the entries in the register must be transferred to the destination`: a yes or no for a stated destination, not a place |

Read and left as they are, because the polarity and the subject agree: `Under section 116(1), may I inspect ...` (the function states the correlative duty, "must be open to the person's inspection", and JUST TRUE means I may); `Under section 113(6B), may a traded company drop my old information ...` (the function says the company "need not include or retain" it); `Under section 120A(6), do the protection rules affect ...` (the function answers JUST FALSE when nothing in the section affects the use of the information, which matches); `Under section 136(1), is the subsidiary barred ...` asks for the bar the function returns (JUST TRUE means barred).

**13 assertions restated in place** in `coa3-cases-seat.l4` (2 for s 112(4), 2 for s 117(1), 3 for s 124(1), 3 for s 135(2), 1 for s 146(5), 2 for s 150(4)): each now carries the new question name and ends `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12: was <the original assertion text>; <reason>`. The counts did not move: 212 cases, 1173 assertions in the deposit.

## Coverage (coa3)

One row per unit of text in lines 1414-2021: a heading, a section heading, a subsection, a paragraph or a table cell, one row each, with exactly one disposition: `encoded`, `inert` (allowed only for the three reasons of the brief: 1 not operative, such as a heading; 2 machinery, such as a power to make regulations or a commencement, extent or savings clause; 3 text shown repealed or blank; in this range every `inert` row is reason 1 or reason 2, and the rows that are a row of dots carry the separate disposition `repealed or blank in this text`), `fork` (an ambiguity, with the fork id in the next section) or `repealed or blank in this text`. There is no out-of-scope row and no deferred row.

The table is generated by `coverage.py` (not shipped) from the text and from a hand-written map; the generator fails if a non-blank line is in no unit or a unit has no row. It is kept at the top of the notes because the brief asks for it; every `where` cell names the L4 function by its prefix up to the first words.

**Audit counts.**

```
non-blank lines in range 1414-2021: 608
units counted by the script (lines/cells, before terms): 621
rows including defined-term rows: 654
lines covered by some unit: 608; lines not covered: []
rows mapped: 654; GAPS (no mapping): 0
  encoded                            419
  inert                              111
  fork                               97
  repealed or blank in this text     27
```

In words: the range has 608 non-blank lines; the script counted 621 units (a line can hold several cells, such as the two columns of the table in s 129(2)), which is the number of rows before defined terms; 33 more rows were added for defined terms (a quoted term in a definition line, such as the three in s 113(9), or a term the text defines without quotation marks), making 654 rows, and 0 units had no row. The brief asks for the units counted and the rows before and after: **621 units counted; 621 rows before terms; 654 rows after**.

## Fork register (coa3)

Every ambiguity, the readings seen with the text that licenses each, and the one taken. The rulings of 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05 govern what they cover; a site they leave to the encoder (an "unless" once met, a bare "must" not done, a duty whose "if" is unmet) gets a line here for the human reviewer to overrule. F1 to F18 are forks taken; F19 to F23 are answers flipped to NOTHING by the name-test sweep of 2026-11-12; F24 to F34 are sites that sweep listed and did not change; F35 is the repair of the "without reasonable excuse" offences of the same date.

| id | provision | function(s) | the readings seen, and the text that licenses each | reading taken |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | s 113(2), lines 1427-1433 | `s 113(2) (first version) the register must contain the entry`; `s 113(2) (second version) the register must contain the entry`; `s 113(2A) ...` | The text prints subsection (2) twice. First version (lines 1427-1430): "There must be entered in the register— (a) the names and addresses of the members, (b) the date on which each person was registered as a member, and (c) the date at which any person ceased to be a member." Second version (lines 1431-1433): "There must be entered in the register, in respect of each person who is a member— (a) the required information (see sections 113A and 113B), and (b) the date on which the person was registered as a member", followed by (2A) "Where a person ceases to be a member there must be entered in the register the date at which the person's membership ceased" (line 1434). Reading 1: the first is the former text and the second, with (2A), replaces it. It is licensed by the text around it: s 112(4) (line 1420) and ss 113A to 113I (lines 1461-1520) speak of "the required information", which only the second version uses. Reading 2: both are in force together (they overlap and differ, so the text cannot mean both without contradiction in (a) and (c)). Reading 3: the first is in force and the second not yet. The text marks neither as repealed or prospective. | Both versions are encoded, each in its own function; `s 113(2A)` goes with the second. The seat layer has no question on s 113(2) itself; its question on s 113(2A) belongs with the second version (reading 1), "assumed, not ruled". |
| F2 | ss 113J and 115, lines 1522-1531 and 1547-1556 | `s 113J(1)` ... `s 113J(6)` (this module); `s 115(1)` ... (inspection module) | The text has two sections headed "Index of members". s 113J(1): "an index of the names or titles of the members"; s 113J(3): "the same details of a person's name or title as are entered in the register of members"; s 115(1): "an index of the names of the members"; s 115(3): "a sufficient indication to enable the account of that member in the register to be readily found". Reading 1: s 113J is the later text and replaces s 115 (113J is placed with the "required information" sections, and uses "titles", which s 113A(3) introduces). Reading 2: both apply. Reading 3: s 115 applies. | Both sections are encoded, each in its own function; the seat layer exposes both (`Under section 113J(1)` and `Under section 115(1)`) and a caller who must choose should prefer s 113J (reading 1), "assumed, not ruled". |
| F3 | ss 113D(3), 113E(3), 113F(2), 148(7) | `s 113D(3) the last day of the period of months beginning with a date` and the functions that call it | The text says "within the period of two months beginning with the date on which ..." and does not say how a period of months is counted. Reading 1 (taken): the period ends on the day before the corresponding date of the later month, and where that month has no such date it ends on the last day of the month (5 January to 4 March; 31 December 2022 to 28 February 2023). Reading 2: the period ends on the corresponding date itself (5 January to 5 March). Reading 3: a month is a fixed number of days (30 or 31). The Act's own text in my ranges carries no definition; a rule of counting is outside the sources. The number of days of a period "of 28 days beginning with" (s 148(7)) is counted with the first day included (day 28 is the last). | Reading 1. |
| F4 | s 113A(1) and s 113B, lines 1462-1464 and 1470-1472 | `s 113A(1) the item is required information about a member who is an individual`; `s 113B the item is required information about a body corporate or a firm that is a legal person` | "The required information about a member who is an individual is— (a) name; (b) a service address." Reading 1 (taken): "is" defines the term, as "means" would, so any other item is not required information (JUST FALSE). Reading 2: no "means" or "only" closes the list, so any other item is NOTHING. | Reading 1: the sentence is the definition of "the required information", which the rest of the chapter uses as a defined term. |
| F5 | s 113G(4)(b)(i) and s 113I(4)(b)(i), lines 1500 and 1518 | `s 113G(4)`, `s 113I(4)` | "imprisonment for a term not exceeding the general limit in a magistrates' court": the number is not in the Act's text in my ranges. s 1131 (lines 16146-16148) fixes "six months" only "in relation to an offence committed before 2 May 2022". Reading 1 (taken): the limit is an INPUT (a `Period`) that the caller supplies from the sentencing law, never guessed. Reading 2: six months throughout (not supported: s 1131(2) limits it by date). | Reading 1: input. |
| F6 | s 112(4), line 1420 | `s 112(4) the person becomes a member under s 112(2) although the entered name is not in the form required by s 113A` | "Where an individual's name is entered ... but is not in the form required by section 113A, that does not affect the person becoming a member by virtue of subsection (2)." The function answers whether s 112(4) saves the membership. Reading 1 (taken): where the "where" fails (the name is in the right form, or no name is entered), the answer is JUST FALSE under ruling 2026-11-05 (b): s 112(4) does not apply; s 112(2) may still make the person a member (use the function for s 112(2)). Reading 2: NOTHING, because JUST FALSE here could be misread as "the person is not a member". | Reading 1, with the comment on each branch. |
| F7 | ss 113D(3), 113E(3), 113J(2) and the later "within N days" provisions | the "in time" functions | A period the text fixes: outside it the answer is JUST FALSE (ruling 2026-11-05 (a)). The function takes the day the act was done. An act done BEFORE the period opens (information provided before the person became a member; notice of a change before the change) is not mentioned: NOTHING (silent). Reading 2: such an act is also in time (JUST TRUE), since a person who gives the information early has plainly not been late. | Reading 1: NOTHING, because "within the period beginning with" does not say that an earlier act counts; s 113D(2)(a) deals with information already provided. |
| F8 | ss 114(1) and 116(4), lines 1534-1537 and 1564-1571 | `s 114(1) the register of members is kept at a place that satisfies s 114(1)`; `s 116(4) the request contains the information that s 116(4) requires` | A bare "must": "must be kept available for inspection— (a) at its registered office, or (b) at a place specified in regulations under section 1136" and "The request must contain the following information". The brief leaves "a bare must not done" to the encoder (ruling of 2026-11-05, six questions decided case by case). Reading 1 (taken): the function answers whether the duty is met, so a register kept elsewhere, or a request missing an item, is JUST FALSE. Reading 2: NOTHING, because "must" is not a flat negative and the text does not say what follows. | Reading 1. |
| F9 | ss 121 and 128(1), lines 1636 and 1681-1684 | `s 121 the entry relating to the former member may be removed from the register`; `s 128(1) the liability is not enforceable` | "after the expiration of ten years from the date on which he ceased to be a member"; "not enforceable more than ten years after the date on which the entry was made". The text does not say how a period of years "from" a date is counted. Reading 1 (taken): the date itself is not counted, so ten years from 5 January 2013 expire at the end of 5 January 2023 and the first day "after" is 6 January 2023 (`add years` the date 10, and the answer is TRUE on a later day). Reading 2: the period expires on 4 January 2023 and 5 January 2023 is already "after". For a date of 29 February `add years` gives 28 February in a common year (the library clamps). | Reading 1. |
| F10 | s 124(2), line 1663 | `s 124(2) the company must be entered in the register as the member holding those shares` | "Subject to subsection (1), where a company holds shares as treasury shares the company must be entered in the register as the member holding those shares." Reading 1 (taken): subsection (1) deals with shares cancelled forthwith, which are not held as treasury shares, so the "where" is the only gate. Reading 2: where s 124(1)(a) applies s 124(2) is displaced (a function that took the answer of s 124(1)(a) as a second input would return JUST FALSE for it). | Reading 1. |
| F11 | s 117(1), line 1573 | `s 117(1) the company acted within five working days` | "within five working days": the Act's definition of working day is s 1173(1) (the keel's, which needs the calendar of bank holidays); the function takes the number of working days after the day of receipt (the day of receipt is day 0 and the fifth working day after it is day 5, in time). Reading 2: the day of receipt counts as the first working day, so day 4 is the last day in time. | Reading 1: "within five working days" of receipt counts from the day after. |
| F12 | s 129(5), line 1738 and s 1(1) (the keel's) | `s 129(5) the reference is to be read as a reference to an overseas branch register kept under s 129` | "(unless the context otherwise requires)": an "unless" once it is met (one of the six questions the ruling of 2026-11-05 leaves to the encoder). Reading 1 (taken): NOTHING, because whether the context requires otherwise is a judgment the text leaves to the reader (the keel's s 1(1) does the same). Reading 2: JUST FALSE, as for any "unless" condition that is met. | Reading 1. |
| F13 | s 136(2), lines 1792-1794 | `s 136(2) the exception is provided for in the provision` | "The exceptions are provided for in— section 138 (subsidiary acting as personal representative or trustee), and section 141 (subsidiary acting as authorised dealer in securities)." But s 137(1) also lets a body corporate that already held shares "continue to be a member of the company", and s 136(1) begins "Except as provided by this Chapter". Reading 1 (taken): the list names two sections, not closed by "only", so another provision of the Chapter answers NOTHING; and `s 136(1)(a)` treats ss 137(1), 138(1) and 141(1) all as exceptions ("Except as provided by this Chapter"). Reading 2: ss 138 and 141 are the only exceptions and s 137 is not one. | Reading 1. |
| F14 | s 137(1), lines 1795-1800 | `s 137(1) the body corporate may continue to be a member of the company` | "Where a body corporate became a holder of shares ... (a) before the relevant date, or (b) ... in circumstances in which the prohibition in section 23(1) of the Companies Act 1985 ... did not apply, or (c) ... in circumstances in which the prohibition in section 136 did not apply, it may continue to be a member." When none of the limbs holds, reading 1 (taken, ruling of 2026-11-05 (b)): JUST FALSE for "s 137(1) permits it to continue", because the "where" fails (the subsection does not help); reading 2: NOTHING, because the body corporate may still be a member under s 138 or s 141, which the function does not ask. The commencement date of the Chapter, and whether the earlier prohibition applied, are inputs. | Reading 1. |
| F15 | ss 138(1) (lines 1808-1811) and 148(3)(b) (line 1932) | `s 138(1) the prohibition in section 136 does not apply to the subsidiary`; `s 148(3) the nomination ceases to have effect on the event` | An "unless" or "otherwise than" once it is met (one of the six sites the ruling of 2026-11-05 leaves to the encoder): s 138(1) "does not apply where the subsidiary is concerned only as trustee ... unless, in the latter case, the holding company or a subsidiary of it is beneficially interested under the trust"; s 148(3)(b) "the making of an order for the winding up of the body otherwise than for the purposes of reconstruction". Reading 1 (taken): when the carve-out is met the answer is JUST FALSE (the subsidiary trustee with a beneficial interest of the group is not excepted; the winding-up order for reconstruction does not end the nomination), because the text names the carve-out in its own words. Reading 2: NOTHING, because the carve-out only withdraws the exception or the event and says nothing of what then follows. | Reading 1. |
| F16 | s 141(3), lines 1849-1854 | `s 141(3) the business is an excluded business` | "The following are excluded businesses— (a) ... (e)": a list without "only" or "means". Reading 1: the list is not closed, so a business that is none of the five answers NOTHING (the rule of 2026-10-29: "lists without closing the list"); but then s 141(2)(c) ("does not carry on an excluded business") can never be satisfied, because a business outside the five can never be shown not to be excluded, and s 141(1) could never answer JUST TRUE. Reading 2 (taken): the list defines "excluded business" for s 141(2)(c), as s 113A(1) defines "the required information" (F4), so any other business is JUST FALSE. The test run found the problem: the first seat case for s 141(1) (a dealer with none of the five businesses) failed under reading 1, answering NOTHING where the section plainly lets a bona fide dealer qualify. | Reading 2. |
| F17 | ss 148(4)(b) and 150(7), lines 1937-1939 and 1973-1975 | `s 148(4)(b) the order or proceeding is the making of an order for winding up for the purposes of s 148(3)`; `s 150(7) the right is a right conferred by the nomination for the purposes of s 150` | "the reference to the making of an order for winding up is to— (i) ..., or (ii) ..." and "References in this section to the rights conferred by the nomination are to— (a) ..., and (b) ...". Reading 1 (taken): "is to" and "are to" say what the reference means, so anything else is JUST FALSE; the contrast in s 148(4)(a) ("the reference to bankruptcy includes") supports the difference. Reading 2: no "means" or "only" closes the list, so anything else is NOTHING. | Reading 1. |
| F18 | s 147(2), line 1917 | `s 147(2) the request was made and the address provided before the nomination was made` | "This must be done before the nomination is made." The function takes the two days. Reading 1 (taken): the request must be on an earlier day than the nomination (a request on the same day is not "before"). Reading 2: a request made earlier on the same day is before it. The text gives no time of day. | Reading 1. |

Reading 2 (the earlier answer): JUST FALSE, a failed "if" read as the text's own no. | Reading 1: NOTHING. |

| F24 | s 113A(3)(a), lines 1466-1467 | `s 113A(3)(a) the requirement to provide the name may be satisfied by providing the title instead` | Listed, not changed (name-test sweep of 2026-11-12). A "may" function whose gate is "Where": ruling (b) of the Guide's "Periods, where and scope limits" paragraph (and ruling (c) of the 2026-11-05 delegated ruling) says JUST FALSE when it fails, and the Guide's line on grants (NOTHING where the text limits a grant in its own wording) points the other way. | F35 | ss 113(7), 113H(1) and 113H(2), lines 1453 and 1504-1505 | `s 113(7) the person commits an offence`; `s 113H(1) the person commits an offence by a false statement in purported compliance with s 113D or s 113E`; `s 113H(2) the person commits an offence by a false statement in purported compliance with a notice under s 113F` | Repair of 2026-11-12, at the lead's request, so that the three sites that word an offence "if ... without reasonable excuse" agree (s 86(3) and s 167L(1) answer JUST FALSE for a default with a reasonable excuse). The text: "If, without reasonable excuse, a company makes default in complying with this section an offence is committed by—" and "A person commits an offence if, in purported compliance with section 113D or 113E and without reasonable excuse, the person makes a statement that is misleading, false or deceptive in a material particular". Reading 1 (taken): the offence is introduced by "if" and the excuse sits inside the gate, so a default or statement with a reasonable excuse answers JUST FALSE (the Guide's "In short": an unmet "if"). Reading 2 (the earlier answer): NOTHING, the excuse read as an exception outside the offence, which the text limits in its own wording. `s 113G(1)-(2)` ("A person who, without reasonable excuse, fails to comply with section 113D or 113E commits an offence") is not changed: its excuse sits in a "who" clause, which the Guide treats as a limit in the provision's own wording, so it keeps NOTHING; if a lawyer wants the four sites to agree, the same other reading applies to it. | Reading 1: JUST FALSE for ss 113(7), 113H(1), 113H(2). |

## Jural relations (coa3)

One row per provision that creates a relation (Hohfeld: claim and duty; privilege and no-claim; power and liability; immunity and disability), with the L4 rule that encodes it. A provision that creates none (a definition, a counting rule, a blank) is listed once as `none`.

| provision | holder of the right, privilege, power or immunity | bearer of the correlative duty, no-right, liability or disability | kind | L4 rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| s 112(1) | a subscriber of the memorandum: to become a member on registration and be entered in the register | the Company: duty to enter the subscriber | claim and duty | `s 112(1) the subscriber must be entered as a member in the register of members` |
| s 112(1) (deemed agreement) | none (a deeming provision: the subscriber is deemed to have agreed) | none | none | `s 112(1) the subscriber is deemed to have agreed to become a member of the company` |
| s 112(2) | none (a status rule: who is a member) | none | none | `s 112(2) the person is a member of the company`; `s 112 the person is a member of the company` |
| s 112(3), s 112A | none (blank in this text) | none | none | (blank) |
| s 112(4) | the individual whose name is entered in a form other than s 113A requires: immunity of membership from the form of the entry | the Company: disability (cannot treat the person as a non-member for that reason) | immunity and disability | `s 112(4) the person becomes a member under s 112(2) ...` |
| s 113(1) | no claim-holder is named (enforced by the offence in s 113(7)) | the Company: duty to keep a register of its members | duty (the correlative claim lies with whoever may prosecute; not named here) | `s 113(1) the company must keep a register of its members`; regulative rule `... (duty)` |

## Marginal readings for a lawyer (coa3)

The two readings the rule of 2026-10-29 leaves open (docs/design-question-false-or-nothing.md), taken here as the status quo:

- **(a)** a failed "if" counts as the text's no, clause by clause, within the provision that holds the "if" (an inference: "if A then B" does not itself say "if not A then not B"), and flat negatives count as the text saying no. **The other reading:** the failed "if" or the flat negative answers NOTHING, because the text says what follows when the condition holds and is silent when it does not.
- **(b)** a prohibition or grant limited by its own wording (a "who ... without reasonable excuse" clause, who is in default, having a share capital, subject to the provisions of this Act) answers NOTHING outside the limit. **The other reading:** it answers JUST FALSE outside the limit, because the limited provision is the text's whole account of the case.

### Reading a result: FALSE and NOTHING (coa3)

Every decision function in layer 1 answers `MAYBE BOOLEAN` (a few answer a date, a number, or a typed `Penalty`), so there are three answers, and a caller must never turn NOTHING into FALSE.

- **JUST TRUE** means the text says yes to the case in its own words: a duty or a right that applies, an offence that is committed, a thing that is in time.
- **JUST FALSE ("a no")** means the text says no in its own words, and the branch carries `-- FALSE-kind (the text says no in its own words): "<the quoted words>"`. In this range that is one of: a "means" or "is" definition that the case fails (`s 113A(2)`, `s 120A(2)`, `s 139(2)`); a condition the text introduces with "if", "where", "in the case of", "so long as", "unless", "until", "when", "on" or "upon" that the case fails (the gate of almost every offence section: "If default is made in complying with this section"), with two exceptions: a function named for a grant or a power ("may ...") whose "if" gate fails answers NOTHING, not JUST FALSE (forks F19 to F23; the name-test sweep of 2026-11-12), and an offence introduced by "if" answers JUST FALSE for a default or statement with a reasonable excuse (s 113(7), s 113H(1) and (2); fork F35); a period the text fixes that has run ("within 14 days", "within the period of two months beginning with"); a flat negative ("does not apply", "cannot be a member", "is not enforceable", "need not", "no notice ... shall be entered"); or a "must" that names what the duty is and the case does not meet it (fork F8). In this subject a "no" answers the exact question in the function's name: for example `s 113(7) the person commits an offence` is JUST FALSE when no default was made or the default had a reasonable excuse (fork F35), never because the person is a member and not an officer.
- **NOTHING ("not covered")** means the text does not decide the case, and the branch carries `-- NOTHING-kind (<why>)`, where <why> is one of: **silent**; **list not closed** ("any of the following", "in particular", a list with no "only"); **includes** ("the reference to bankruptcy includes"); **undefined term**; **points outside the text** (s 1121, s 853E(6), s 1159, s 1173(1), Schedule 5, regulations under s 120A or s 1136, an Act that is not among the sources); **limited by its own wording** ("without reasonable excuse", "A company having a share capital", "who is in default", "Subject to the provisions of this Act", "a person who holds shares on behalf of another person"). Most offence provisions answer NOTHING for an officer who is not "in default" and for anyone who is not the company or an officer, because the text limits the offence in its own words and says nothing of the case outside the limit. A person who has a reasonable excuse answers NOTHING under s 113G(1) and (2) ("A person who, without reasonable excuse, fails ..."), but JUST FALSE under the "if" offences s 113(7), s 113H(1) and s 113H(2) (fork F35).
- **Three-valued inputs.** A seam input is a `MAYBE BOOLEAN`: JUST TRUE or JUST FALSE as the other section says, NOTHING where it is not known; NOTHING passes through as NOTHING (the seat asks "yes", "no" or "not known").
- **The two readings left to a lawyer** are listed branch by branch in `## Marginal readings for a lawyer (coa3)`.

**Every branch that rests on one of the two readings** (296 branches: 188 on reading (a), 108 on reading (b)); the "rests on" column classifies the quoted words, and a lawyer should read the quoted words, not the class. A branch that is a plain `means` statement of a defined term is listed under (a) when the heuristic cannot tell it from a "no"; the quoted words show which it is. The lines are lines of the layer-1 module named, as they stand in the deposit.

| coa3-part9-rights.l4:260 | `s 147(4) the right under s 147(3) is unaffected by Parts 3 and 4 of Schedule 5` | JUST TRUE when the input says the company has taken no Schedule 5 steps (JUST FALSE) | the text says the right is "subject to" Parts 3 and 4 of Schedule 5, which operate only where "the company may take steps"; with none taken I read the right as unaffected. The other reading: NOTHING, because the text does not itself say the Parts are idle when no steps are taken. |

### Name-test sweep of 2026-11-12

The lead's sweep asked of every function whose name contains "may", "permitted", "power", "entitled" or "authorised": is it named for a grant or a power and does it answer JUST FALSE because an "if" gate failed? I read all 30 such functions of the five layer-1 modules. 8 of them never answer JUST FALSE (`s 113F(1)`, `s 125(2)`, `s 131(4)`, `s 135(1)`, `s 146(2)`, `s 147(6)(a)`, `s 148(2)`, `s 148(8)`), so the sweep does not touch them. The other 22 sort as follows. Gates equivalent to "where", "when" or "upon" ("in the case of", "so long as", "while", "on payment", "on such an application", "after ...", "on ...") go with the listed group; that is assumed, not ruled. (s 118(3), "In the case of", and s 137(3), "So long as", were flipped in a first pass and moved to the listed group at the lead's direction; s 121, "after the expiration of ten years", is listed for the same reason, and its answer, JUST FALSE, is unchanged either way.)

| flipped to NOTHING | kept JUST FALSE (gate closed by the text) | listed: "may" with where/when/upon | listed: "entitled" |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 5 | 6 | 10 | 1 |

**Flipped to NOTHING** (a grant or power whose "if" gate failed; each has one fork line, F19 to F23, in the fork register and in the table above; the other reading is JUST FALSE, a failed "if" read as the text's own no):
- `s 129(1) the company may cause to be kept in the country or territory a branch register of members resident there` (F19)
- `s 117(3)(b) the court may order the company's costs on the application to be paid in whole or in part by the person who made the request` (F20)
- `s 117(4) the court may direct that the company is not to comply with any such request` (F21)
- `s 122(3) the bearer of a share warrant may be deemed a member of the company, to the full extent or for the purposes the articles define` (F22)
- `s 125(1) the applicant may apply to the court for rectification of the register` (F23)

**Kept JUST FALSE:**
- `s 134(1) the court may exercise the same jurisdiction as a court in the United Kingdom` and `s 134(2) the offence may be prosecuted summarily before the tribunal`: the text closes the gate with "only" ("This section extends only to those countries and territories").
- `s 133(2) a transaction with respect to the shares may be registered in any other register`: the text is a flat negative ("No transaction ... may be registered in any other register"), a bar that the name merely words as a "may".
- `s 126 notice of a trust may be entered on the register of members or be received by the registrar`: a flat negative ("No notice of any trust ... shall be entered"), the same.
- `s 148(7) the company may make the enquiry of the person`: a bar ("Such an enquiry is not to be made of a person more than once in any twelve-month period").
- `s 147(5) the nominated person is taken to have agreed that documents or information may be sent or supplied to him by means of a website`: a deeming, not a grant or a power; the provision's own question.

**Listed, not changed: "may" with a "where", "when" or "upon" gate, or an equivalent** (forks F24 to F33; ruling (b) says JUST FALSE, the Guide's line on grants says NOTHING; a question for the maintainer or a lawyer):
- `s 113A(3)(a) the requirement to provide the name may be satisfied by providing the title instead` (F24, "Where")
- `s 113A(3)(b) the title may be entered in the register of members instead of the forename and surname` (F25, "Where")
- `s 116(2) the person may require a copy of the register of members or of any part of it` (F26, "on payment")
- `s 118(3) the court may by order compel an immediate inspection or direct that the copy required be sent to the person requesting it` (F27, "In the case of")
- `s 121 the entry relating to the former member may be removed from the register` (F28, "after the expiration of ten years"; see also F9)
- `s 125(3) the court may decide the question` (F29, "On such an application")
- `s 137(1) the body corporate may continue to be a member of the company` (F30, "Where"; see also F14)
- `s 137(3) an allotment to the body corporate of fully paid shares in the company may be validly made by way of capitalisation of reserves` (F31, "So long as")
- `s 145(2) the act is done, or may be done, by or in relation to the nominated person instead of the member` (F32, "Where", through s 145(1))
- `s 152(1) the member may exercise some only of the rights, or exercise them in different ways` (F33, "Where")

**Listed, not changed: "entitled"** (F34): `s 152(4) the company is entitled to assume that the member is exercising all his rights and is exercising them in the same way`.

Seat questions that follow the flipped functions answer NOTHING in the same cases: `Under section 117(3)`, `Under section 122(3)`, `Under section 125(1)` and `Under section 129(1)`. 4 seat assertions and 11 layer-1 assertions (3 for s 129(1), 2 for s 117(3)(b), 2 for s 117(4), 1 for s 122(3), 1 for s 125(1), and 1 each for s 118(3) and s 137(3), which are back to JUST FALSE because those two sites are listed and not flipped) are restated in place with `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12: was ...`.

**Repair of 2026-11-12 (fork F35).** At the lead's request, s 113(7), s 113H(1) and s 113H(2), whose offence is introduced by "if" with the reasonable excuse inside the gate, now answer JUST FALSE for a default or statement with a reasonable excuse, as the same wording does in other parts; the other reading is NOTHING. s 113G(1) and (2) ("A person who, without reasonable excuse, fails ...") are left at NOTHING. 3 layer-1 assertions and 2 seat assertions are restated in place.

Seams inside this range (my own functions pass their answers to each other as `MAYBE BOOLEAN` inputs, so a caller composes them): `s 113(9)` to `s 113(6A)` and `s 113(6B)`; `s 113J(1)` to `s 113J(2)`; `s 115(1)` to `s 115(2)`; `s 129(2)` to `s 129(1)`; `s 134(3)` to `s 134(1)` and `s 134(2)`; `s 137(1)`, `s 138(1)`, `s 141(1)` to `s 136(1)`; `s 139(1)` to `s 138(2)` and `s 140`; `s 141(2)` and `s 141(3)` to `s 141(1)`; `s 142(1)` to `s 142(2)`; `s 145(1)` to `s 145(2)`; `s 146(1)` to `s 146(2)`; `s 149(1)` to `s 149(2)` and `s 149(3)`; `s 137(1)` to `s 137(3)`.

What other parts take from this range (PLAN.md section 6, row coa3): `s 112 the person is a member of the company` (member, `s 112`); `s 113(1) the company must keep a register of its members` (register of members, `s 113`); `s 129(1) the register is an overseas branch register` (overseas branch register); `s 131(1) the register is the main register` (the main register); `s 146(3) the right is one of the "information rights"` (information rights).
Cross-references in my range that point at sections of other parts and are not answers: `s 113C` to `s 1292` (inert); `s 114` to `s 1136` (an input above); `s 124` to `s 724` (an input above); `s 127` to `s 1080` (an input above); `s 128ZA` to `s 1081` (an input above); `s 140` to `s 57` is a false positive of the plan's script (it is section 57 of the Pension Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993); `s 145` to ss 291 to 293, 303, 310, 314, 319A, 324, 338, 338A, 360AA, 360BA and 423 are the enumeration `s 145(3) right` (named, not computed); `s 146` and `s 147` to `s 1145`, ss 426, 431, 432 and Schedule 5 are named in the enumeration `s 146(3) right` and the input above; `s 153` to ss 314, 338, 338A, 342 and 527 are the enumeration `s 153(1) section of the Companies Act 2006`.

## What the text turns out to say (coa3)

Each finding below was checked against the text file (line numbers are lines of `ukpga-2006-46.txt`) and, where it concerns the code, against the module at the file and line named.

1. **Subsection (2) of section 113 is printed twice**, at lines 1427-1430 and 1431-1433, and the two do not say the same thing: the first has the names and addresses of the members, the date each was registered and the date each ceased to be a member; the second has "the required information (see sections 113A and 113B)" and the registration date, with the cessation date moved to a new subsection (2A) at line 1434. The text marks neither as repealed or not yet in force. Both are encoded, side by side (fork F1).
2. **Two sections are headed "Index of members"**: s 113J (lines 1522-1531) and s 115 (lines 1547-1556). Each begins "Every company having more than 50 members must keep an index" (lines 1523 and 1548). They differ in their later words (s 113J speaks of "names or titles"; s 115(1) of "names"), and both have an offence section of their own (s 113J(5) and s 115(5)). Both are encoded (fork F2).
3. **Rows of dots.** In this rendering s 112(3), s 122(4) and s 129(6) are each a row of dots, and twelve whole sections (112A at lines 1423-1424 and 128A to 128K at lines 1692-1713) are a heading followed by a row of dots. These are the 27 rows with the disposition `repealed or blank in this text`; nothing is encoded for them and no rule is invented to fill them (Chapter 2A, the option to keep information on the central register, is therefore empty here; only s 128ZA, which has text, speaks of it).
4. **s 122 has no subsection (2)**: the numbering runs (1), (3), (4), (5), (6) (lines 1639-1646).
5. **s 136(2) names two exceptions, but s 137(1) is a third.** "The exceptions are provided for in section 138 ... and section 141", yet s 137(1) lets a body corporate that held shares before the prohibition "continue to be a member" (lines 1792-1800); s 136(1) itself begins "Except as provided by this Chapter" (fork F13).
6. **The penalty clauses are not alike.** On summary conviction in England and Wales, s 113G(4)(b)(i) (line 1500) says "the general limit in a magistrates' court"; s 119(3)(b)(i) (line 1598) says "twelve months". In Scotland, s 113G(4) says 12 months and adds a daily default fine, while s 119(3)(b)(ii) says six months and has none (and puts Northern Ireland with Scotland). The number behind "the general limit in a magistrates' court" is not in the sources: s 1131(2) (line 16148) says to read it as "six months" only for an offence committed before 2 May 2022 (fork F5).
7. **Section 153(2) fixes two numerical thresholds** that a request must meet: "made by at least 100 persons" (line 2003) and a total paid up (the sum in paragraph (f)), "divided by the number of persons making the request, is not less than £100" (lines 2017-2020). Both are tested at the boundary: 100 persons against 99, and an average of exactly £100 against £99.99 (the total in pounds is the input, so the division is the code's; the seat cases use 100 persons with 10,000 against 9,999).
8. **The table in s 129(2)(b)** lists 26 countries or territories (13 rows of two at lines 1720-1732). It is encoded as a vertical list of 26 strings, generated from the text by script and checked by `srcrefs.py`.
9. **"Without reasonable excuse" and "who is in default" are limits on the offences.** "Without reasonable excuse" appears in s 113(7) (line 1453), s 113G(1) and (2) (lines 1494-1495) and s 113H(1) and (2) (lines 1504-1505), and "who is in default" in 14 lines of the range. The text makes the offence turn on them but never says that a person outside them is innocent. For "who is in default" a person outside the limit answers NOTHING (reading (b) below). For the excuse the sites differ by their opening words: s 113(7), s 113H(1) and s 113H(2) are introduced by "If" and answer JUST FALSE for a default or statement with a reasonable excuse, as the same wording does in other parts (fork F35); s 113G(1) and (2) are introduced by "A person who" and answer NOTHING.
10. **The text counts two months, and does not count ten years.** ss 113D(3), 113E(3), 113F(2) and 148(7) say "the period of two months beginning with the date" (and one month, and 28 days): the starting date is day one, so the last day is the day before the corresponding date (5 January 2023 + two months: last day 4 March 2023, tested). ss 121 and 128(1) say only "after the expiration of ten years from the date" and "more than ten years after the date", with no rule for counting; I took the date as not counted, so the tenth anniversary is not yet "after" (fork F9, reading 1).

## What the stable binary does not accept (coa3)

Where a skill or the brief describes a construct the stable binary rejects, the binary governs. What I met, checked on 1.6.110 (main, e51181d, 2026-11-11); the first bullet was also checked on 1.6.109, where it prints the same:

- **`#ASSERT` over a regulative rule is an error, not a pass or a fail.** `#ASSERT `s 113(1) the company must keep a register of its members (duty)` EQUALS FULFILLED` prints `eval/unsupported-equality` ("assertion could not be evaluated: Trying to check equality on types that do not support it ... PARTY Company"), and `check.sh` counts that as a failed assertion. So every regulative rule in my tests files is run with `#TRACE` (the FULFILLED or BREACHED outcome is printed, not asserted), and the same duty is also encoded as a decision function over the facts (for example `s 113D(3) the person provided the information within the period of two months`), which is asserted. `check.sh` does not see a different trace outcome, so I compared the printed traces of the four tests files that have regulative rules (members, inspection, branch, rights) on 1.6.110 and 1.6.109 by hand: identical. The subsidiary module has no regulative rule.
- **`length` is not defined**: `#EVAL length (LIST 1, 2)` prints `check/unknown-identifier` on 1.6.110. The count of a list is `count`, which I use.
- **`ROR`** (either of two actions fulfils the duty) works in a `DEONTIC` rule; `s 117` uses it.
- **Brief rules followed, not re-tested**: no `REFUSE`, `BECAUSE` only after `LEST BREACH`; no sets, no `TYPICALLY`, no opaque `DECLARE`, no section `GIVEN`; `Date day month year`; every `NOT` in brackets; one-line `#ASSERT`s; vertical `LIST` blocks.

How the 1173 divide: 961 assertions in the five tests files (242 members, 237 inspection, 121 branch, 161 subsidiary, 200 rights), and 212 in `coa3-cases-seat.l4` for the 106 seat questions (each question has at least one case: `gen_cases.py` fails otherwise; 93 of the cases expect JUST TRUE, 65 JUST FALSE and 33 NOTHING, that is "not covered"; the other 21 expect a date or a penalty). The brief also asks that `l4 run` on each file shows no `DiagnosticSeverity_Error` or `_Warning`: none of the files prints one, and `l4 check` prints no "follows code on its line" or "is at the end of the file" warning.
